| mastercard-008 | OAuth 1.0a mechanics: a 97-character non-secret consumer key plus a 2048-bit RSA private signing key (RSA only), signature method fixed to RSA-SHA256 with the oauth_body_hash extension; the portal can generate the key pair in the browser so Mastercard never holds the private key. | developer | Using OAuth 1.0a to Access Mastercard APIs | Mastercard Developers | https://developer.mastercard.com/platform/documentation/authentication/using-oauth-1a-to-access-mastercard-apis/index.md | n.d. | 2026-09-25 | Verified |  | Quote: "Consumer keys are 97-character identifiers for client applications consuming Mastercard APIs." · 'The signing key is a 2048-bit private RSA key'; 'It must be RSA-SHA256'. CSR doc: 'For OAuth 1.0a, only RSA keys are supported'. OAuth Keys guide: 'When you choose this option, your browser generates the private key and downloads it for you. Mastercard never sees your private key.' Keys are delivered as PKCS#12 (.p12). · fetch curl · verified 2026-09-25 |

| mastercard-067 | Network Enablement Partner (NEP) is a direct-contract tier for operationally significant Service Providers: a provider may apply, or Mastercard may designate it, after which it must sign an NEP Agreement within 30 days or Customers must stop using it within 180 days (Rules 7.11.1-7.11.2). | join | Mastercard Rules (2 June 2026 edition), Rule 7.11 Network Enablement Partners, PDF p.190-195 | Mastercard International Incorporated | https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf | 2026-06-02 | 2026-09-25 | Verified | https://web.archive.org/web/20260920203706id_/https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf | Quote: "May be designated by the Corporation to be a Network Enablement Partner, when deemed by the Corporation to be operationally significant" · Applicants 'complete and submit the NEP Application form to service_provider@mastercard.com'. ## Contradictions

- Quick Start Guide: key-expiry reminder emails 'are sent 60, 30, 15, 5, and 1 day prior to expiration' · versus · OAuth Keys guide: 'key owners and project admins will get notifications 90, 60, 30, 15, 7, and 1 day(s) before the key expires' · https://developer.mastercard.com/platform/documentation/quick-start-guide/ · https://developer.mastercard.com/platform/documentation/credential-management/oauth-key-management/index.md
- Products catalogue logged out shows 'View All 91' products · versus · llms.txt lists 74 entries under '## Products' and 94 'serviceId:' documentation sets; the API status page lists roughly 110 monitored services · https://developer.mastercard.com/products · https://developer.mastercard.com/llms.txt · https://developer.mastercard.com/api-status
- Maven Central search API (search.maven.org) reports com.mastercard.developer:oauth1-signer latestVersion 1.5.3 dated 2024-03-22 · versus · repo1.maven.org maven-metadata.xml reports <latest>1.6.0</latest> lastUpdated 2026-03-05, matching GitHub release v1.6.0 (2026-03-05); the search index is stale · https://search.maven.org/solrsearch/select?q=g:com.mastercard.developer&rows=30&wt=json · https://repo1.maven.org/maven2/com/mastercard/developer/oauth1-signer/maven-metadata.xml
- 'Working with llms.txt' says llms-full.txt 'stays automatically updated' and the site footer and doc nav link a portal-level llms-full.txt · versus · https://developer.mastercard.com/llms-full.txt returns HTTP 401 and redirects to the portal login, while per-service files such as /bin-lookup/documentation/llms-full.txt are public (200) · https://developer.mastercard.com/platform/documentation/agent-toolkit/working-with-llmstxt/index.md · https://developer.mastercard.com/llms-full.txt
- PyPI shows mastercard-client-encryption 1.23.3 uploaded 2025-06-16 · versus · GitHub releases API shows client-encryption-python v1.23.3 published 2025-12-04 · https://pypi.org/pypi/mastercard-client-encryption/json · https://api.github.com/repos/Mastercard/client-encryption-python/releases/latest
- Rule 7.1 (PDF p.165): 'As of 20 May 2025, Service Providers are no longer designated as Type I TPPs.' · versus · Rule 7.10.2 (PDF p.188) in the same 2 June 2026 edition still prescribes: 'Within 90 days of receiving notification of such Service Provider's designation by the Corporation as a Type I TPP, the TPP must submit TPP Registration Form 919', and US Rule 7.9 (p.369) still lets a Type I TPP sponsor issuing Affiliates. · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf
- Mastercard Developers support page links 'Visit Engage' to https://www.mastercard.com/global/en/business/fintech/fintech-programs/mastercard-engage.html, and the Engage FAQ says 'Learn more at www.mastercard.com/engage'. · versus · The seed https://developer.mastercard.com/engage answers HTTP 401 'Session expired' (curl) / 404 (browser), and https://www.mastercard.com/engage answers Akamai 403 to curl; the two Engage entry points named by Mastercard do not resolve consistently for non-browser clients. · https://developer.mastercard.com/support · https://developer.mastercard.com/engage
- Mastercard's March 26, 2024 press release says the Rules Relief class settlement would reduce and cap U.S. credit interchange for five years with rule changes 'most likely in late 2024 or early 2025'. · versus · Mastercard's FY2025 10-K (Note 19) says that 2024 settlement 'was subsequently denied by the court' and that a revised November 2025 agreement awaits preliminary approval. · https://www.mastercard.com/us/en/news-and-trends/press/2024/march/mastercard-commits-to-lowering-us-interchange-for-small-businesses-and-broader-merchant-community.html · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000013/ma-20251231.htm
- The Federal Reserve Reg II data-collections page presents the calendar-year 2023 report as the latest interchange fee data release. · versus · The Board's 'Average Debit Card Interchange Fee by Payment Card Network' page shows 2024 per-network figures (e.g. Mastercard covered $0.24 / 0.49%). · https://www.federalreserve.gov/paymentsystems/regii-data-collections.htm · https://www.federalreserve.gov/paymentsystems/regii-average-interchange-fee.htm
- FY2025 and FY2024 10-K: 'more than 150 currencies and in more than 220 countries and territories' · versus · mastercard.com About page: 'more than 210 countries and territories today'; newsroom boilerplate (2021, 2022): '200+ countries and territories worldwide' · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000013/ma-20251231.htm · https://www.mastercard.com/global/en/for-the-world/about-us.html · https://www.mastercard.com/news/europe/en/perspectives/en/2021/blog-from-valerie-nowak-why-this-maestro-is-retiring-after-30-years/
- FY2024 10-K: 'approximately 150 million acceptance locations and over 250 million digital access points worldwide' · versus · FY2025 10-K: 'hundreds of millions of acceptance locations and digital access points worldwide' (specific figures dropped) · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139125000011/ma-20241231.htm · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000013/ma-20251231.htm
- Mastercard Engage page: '170+ qualified financial technology partners' · versus · Engage partner directory pagination: '(1-48 of 202)' partners listed · https://www.mastercard.com/engage · https://engagepartners.mastercard.com/english/directory/
- FY2024 10-K: 'We switch approximately 70% of all transactions for Mastercard and Maestro-branded cards' · versus · FY2025 10-K: 'We switch more than 70% of all transactions for Mastercard and Maestro-branded cards' (year-on-year change in wording, not necessarily an error) · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139125000011/ma-20241231.htm · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000013/ma-20251231.htm
- FY2025 10-K card table totals 3,391 million cards (1,083 + 2,134 + 174) 'Excludes Maestro and Cirrus cards' · versus · Q4 2025 earnings release: '3.7 billion Mastercard and Maestro-branded cards' as of December 31, 2025 (different scope: includes Maestro) · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000013/ma-20251231.htm · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000003/ma12312025-exx991xearnings.htm
- Mastercard Rules 3 June 2025 (page 169) requires registration in the 'Mastercard Registration Program (MRP) system using Mastercard Connect' · versus · Mastercard Rules 2 June 2026 contains no '(MRP) system' wording and its summary of changes cites 'GLB 12564 Revised Standards for the Specialty Merchant Registration Program' (7.8.3) · https://www.mastercard.us/content/dam/public/mastercardcom/na/global-site/documents/mastercard-rules.pdf · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf
- Security Rules and Procedures 11 February 2025 (8.3.2) says ECP merchants are monitored 'through the Data Integrity Online application on Mastercard Connect' · versus · Security Rules and Procedures 4 August 2026 (8.3.2) says 'through the Data Integrity application on Mastercard Connect' · https://www.mastercard.us/content/dam/public/mastercardcom/na/global-site/documents/SPME-Manual.pdf · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/SPME-Manual.pdf
- A Wayback capture of the legacy mastercard.us Mastercard Rules URL dated 2026-01-15 serves the 3 June 2025 edition (484 pages) · versus · The mastercard.com rules-pdfs path served the 2 June 2026 edition (503 pages) by 2026-09-20, so the same document title carried two edition dates and page counts depending on URL · https://www.mastercard.us/content/dam/public/mastercardcom/na/global-site/documents/mastercard-rules.pdf · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf
- Chargeback Gurus (2025-10-21) states ECM = 100+ chargebacks and >1.5% for two consecutive months, HECM = 300+ and 3%, with a fine table from USD 1,000 to 200,000 · versus · Mastercard's public Security Rules (4 August 2026, 8.3) give no thresholds or amounts, deferring to 'Edit 2' of the gated Data Integrity Monitoring Program manual · https://www.chargebackgurus.com/blog/mastercard-excessive-chargeback-program · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/SPME-Manual.pdf
- Mastercard Rules 3 June 2025 metadata labels the document 'Type Brand-related standards' · versus · Mastercard Rules 2 June 2026 metadata labels it 'Type Product or Service Guide: Legal and compliance standards' · https://www.mastercard.us/content/dam/public/mastercardcom/na/global-site/documents/mastercard-rules.pdf · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/shared/business/support/rules-pdfs/mastercard-rules.pdf
- SoFi Tech Solutions lists the issuer-initiated P2P transaction-type bulletin as 'GLB 13264.2 — Issuer-Initiated Person-to-Person (P2P) Transactions', effective October 23, 2026 · versus · Marqeta lists the same change as 'GLB 13264.1 - Identifying issuer-initiated person-to-person payment and funding transactions', effective 23 October 2026 (different revision suffix) · https://docs.tech.sofi.com/pro/docs/card-network-mandates · https://www.marqeta.com/docs/developer-guides/card-network-certifications
- Marqeta states the 'April 2026 Mastercard Release 26.Q2' is effective 17 April 2026 · versus · Rivero AG attributes age verification (GLB 11738.1, effective 2 June 2026 per SoFi) to 'Mastercard's 2nd June network processing release' while citing the 'System Release 26.Q2' annex, so the same release label is tied to two effective dates · https://www.marqeta.com/docs/developer-guides/card-network-certifications · https://rivero.tech/blog/mastercard-age-verification-mandate-live-issuers-acquirers · https://docs.tech.sofi.com/pro/docs/card-network-mandates
- Chargeback Gurus (2025-10-21) presents ECP thresholds as stable (ECM 100+/1.5%, HECM 300+/3%) · versus · SLYCE360 reports that under GLB 14127.1 the ECM ratio thresholds 'hold steady through 2028, then step down each January from 2029 through 2031' and monitoring moves to sub-merchant ID from 1 April 2027 · https://www.chargebackgurus.com/blog/mastercard-excessive-chargeback-program · https://www.slyce360.com/mastercard-gmap-explained-what-acquirers-need-to-know-before-april-2027/
- Mastercard Services page: '1.8t loyalty points managed every year', footnoted to 'Mastercard Investor Day, 2024' · versus · Consumer Acquisition & Engagement page: '1.3t loyalty points managed', footnoted to 'Mastercard Investor Day, 2024' · https://www.mastercard.com/us/en/business/services.html · https://www.mastercard.com/us/en/business/consumer-acquisition-and-engagement.html
- Task context supplied to this research says Maestro 'is being retired' · versus · Mastercard 2025 Form 10-K (filed 2026-02-11) still describes Maestro as 'our PIN-based solution that operates globally' with no retirement language · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1141391/000114139126000013/ma-20251231.htm · https://www.mastercard.com/news/search/?q=maestro
- Tokenization page: '4b+ Mastercard transactions worldwide are tokenized each month' sourced 'Mastercard Data, March 2023' · versus · Cybersecurity page: '4b tokenized transactions secured every month' sourced 'Mastercard 2026 data' (same figure, three years apart) · https://www.mastercard.com/us/en/business/payments/consumer-payments/network-processing/tokenization.html · https://www.mastercard.com/us/en/business/cybersecurity-fraud-prevention.html
- BIN Lookup Support page says API uptime and recent issues are 'publicly available' on developer.mastercard.com/api-status. · versus · The api-status URL serves only a client-rendered shell to non-browser fetchers, and Mastercard's separate Atlassian Statuspage shows only '(example)' placeholder components and no incidents ever, so no incident history is verifiably public. · https://developer.mastercard.com/bin-lookup/documentation/support/index.md · https://developer.mastercard.com/api-status · https://mastercard.statuspage.io/
- developer.mastercard.com/api-status is a live per-service status page ('Mastercard Developers performs health checks approximately once per minute.') listing ~100 services · versus · mastercard.statuspage.io, titled 'Mastercard Status', shows 'All Systems Operational' but only placeholder components 'API (example)' and 'Management Portal (example)' · https://developer.mastercard.com/api-status · https://mastercard.statuspage.io/
- Mastercard Developers Support page describes Engage as 'a directory of approved specialists that can implement and build your next solution' · versus · The Engage page defines participants as 'qualified partners' who 'completed Mastercard's product qualification process' and describes a marketplace and portal, not only a directory · https://developer.mastercard.com/support · https://www.mastercard.com/global/en/business/fintech/fintech-programs/mastercard-engage.html
- Brand Center footer reads '© 1994-2025 Mastercard.' and its latest-updates entries are dated December 2023 · versus · Every other Mastercard property fetched (developer portal, mastercard.com business pages, Connect) shows '© 1994-2026' · https://brand.mastercard.com/brandcenter.html · https://www.mastercard.com/us/en/business/support/rules.html

## Claims this research could not verify

- mastercard-097 · Canada domestic consumer credit interchange effective August 1, 2026: Card Present EMV is 0.92% (Core), 1.22% (World), 1.56% (World Elite) and 1.95% (World Legend); Standard is 1.96% to 2.53%; domestic debit Supermarket/Petroleum is 0.15% + CAD 0.05. · could not be re-read by any method on 2026-09-25 · Aug 2026 Canada upcoming-rates PDF is not archived; only the April/May 2026 and October 2025 Canada PDFs have Wayback captures. · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/ca/en/business/support/documents/interchange-criteria-rates-aug12026-upcoming-rates-en.pdf
- mastercard-100 · Australia domestic Mastercard credit interchange effective 17 April 2026 (ex-GST): Base rate is 0.19% for Consumer Standard, 0.27% Consumer Premium, 0.70% Consumer Elite and 0.59% Commercial; Strategic Merchants 1 pays 0.18% on most products. · could not be re-read by any method on 2026-09-25 · Australia credit POS interchange PDF could not be read by any method. · https://www.mastercard.com/content/dam/mccom/aus/data-source/merchant-interchange-rates/Australia-Intracountry-CREDIT-POS-Interchange-Rates.pdf
